/**
 * JSON-RPC 2.0 client implementation for communicating with the Copilot CLI.
 *
 * @since 1.0.0
 */
class JsonRpcClient implements AutoCloseable {

    private static final Logger LOG = Logger.getLogger(JsonRpcClient.class.getName());
    private static final ObjectMapper MAPPER = createObjectMapper();

    private final InputStream inputStream;
    private final OutputStream outputStream;
    private final Socket socket;
    private final Process process;
    private final boolean ownsStreams;
    private final AtomicLong requestIdCounter = new AtomicLong(0);
    private final Map<Long, CompletableFuture<JsonNode>> pendingRequests = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final Map<String, BiConsumer<String, JsonNode>> notificationHandlers = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final ExecutorService readerExecutor;
    private volatile boolean running = true;

    private JsonRpcClient(InputStream inputStream, OutputStream outputStream, Socket socket, Process process) {
        this(inputStream, outputStream, socket, process, false);
    }

    private JsonRpcClient(InputStream inputStream, OutputStream outputStream, Socket socket, Process process,
            boolean ownsStreams) {
        this.inputStream = inputStream;
        this.outputStream = outputStream;
        this.socket = socket;
        this.process = process;
        this.ownsStreams = ownsStreams;
        this.readerExecutor = Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor(r -> {
            Thread t = new Thread(r, "jsonrpc-reader");
            t.setDaemon(true);
            return t;
        });
        startReader();
    }

    static ObjectMapper createObjectMapper() {
        var mapper = new ObjectMapper();
        mapper.registerModule(new JavaTimeModule());
        mapper.configure(DeserializationFeature.FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES, false);
        mapper.configure(SerializationFeature.WRITE_DATES_AS_TIMESTAMPS, false);
        mapper.setDefaultPropertyInclusion(
                JsonInclude.Value.construct(JsonInclude.Include.NON_NULL, JsonInclude.Include.ALWAYS));
        return mapper;
    }

    public static ObjectMapper getObjectMapper() {
        return MAPPER;
    }

    /**
     * Creates a JSON-RPC client using stdio with a process.
     */
    public static JsonRpcClient fromProcess(Process process) {
        return new JsonRpcClient(process.getInputStream(), process.getOutputStream(), null, process);
    }

    /**
     * Creates a JSON-RPC client using TCP socket.
     */
    public static JsonRpcClient fromSocket(Socket socket) throws IOException {
        return new JsonRpcClient(socket.getInputStream(), socket.getOutputStream(), socket, null);
    }

    /**
     * Creates a JSON-RPC client over arbitrary input/output streams. The client
     * takes ownership of the streams and closes them when {@link #close()} is
     * called.
     */
    public static JsonRpcClient fromStreams(InputStream inputStream, OutputStream outputStream) {
        return new JsonRpcClient(inputStream, outputStream, null, null, true);
    }

    /**
     * Registers a handler for JSON-RPC method calls (requests/notifications from
     * server).
     */
    public void registerMethodHandler(String method, BiConsumer<String, JsonNode> handler) {
        notificationHandlers.put(method, handler);
    }

    /**
     * Sends a JSON-RPC request and waits for the response.
     */
    public <T> CompletableFuture<T> invoke(String method, Object params, Class<T> responseType) {

        }
    }

    /**
     * Verifies that setting reasoningEffort alongside streaming=true does not break
     * the streaming pipeline — deltas still arrive and complete successfully.
     *
     * @see Snapshot:
     *      streaming_fidelity/should_emit_streaming_deltas_with_reasoning_effort_configured
     */
    @Test
    void testShouldEmitStreamingDeltasWithReasoningEffortConfigured() throws Exception {
        try (E2ETestContext isolatedContext = E2ETestContext.create()) {
            isolatedContext.configureForTest("streaming_fidelity",
                    "should_emit_streaming_deltas_with_reasoning_effort_configured");

            try (CopilotClient client = isolatedContext.createClient()) {
                CopilotSession session = client
                        .createSession(new SessionConfig().setOnPermissionRequest(PermissionHandler.APPROVE_ALL)
                                .setModel("gpt-5.4").setStreaming(true).setReasoningEffort("high"))
                        .get();

                List<SessionEvent> events = new ArrayList<>();
                session.on(events::add);

                session.sendAndWait(new MessageOptions().setPrompt("What is 15 * 17?")).get(60, TimeUnit.SECONDS);

                // With streaming + reasoning effort, we should still get content deltas
                List<AssistantMessageDeltaEvent> deltaEvents = events.stream()
                        .filter(e -> e instanceof AssistantMessageDeltaEvent).map(e -> (AssistantMessageDeltaEvent) e)
                        .toList();
                assertFalse(deltaEvents.isEmpty(),
                        "Should have received delta events with reasoning effort configured");

                // And a final assistant.message with the answer
                List<AssistantMessageEvent> assistantEvents = events.stream()
                        .filter(e -> e instanceof AssistantMessageEvent).map(e -> (AssistantMessageEvent) e).toList();
                assertFalse(assistantEvents.isEmpty(), "Should have received assistant message events");
                assertTrue(assistantEvents.get(assistantEvents.size() - 1).getData().content().contains("255"),
                    "Response should contain 255");

            session.close();
        }
    }
}

                // Connection torn down (e.g. client closing); ignore.

### Reasoning

***Refining mvn commands**

I need to set up a targeted failsafe for my Maven commands. Using `mvn verify -Pinprocess -DskipTests` likely skips the failsafe. Plus, `-Dtest=...` affects surefire, and if I use `-Dit.test`, it won't fail if none are specified. If I run `mvn failsafe:integration-test failsafe:verify`, I’ll need package classes that aren’t there if the runtime isn't installed. I should just run `mvn verify ... -Dtest=NativeRuntimeLoaderTest` to package and run targeted units and selected integration tests within 10 minutes.*

Thought for 19s
Let me examine the workflows and the current POM structure.

Here's the analysis. The POM structure changed from a single module to a multi-module reactor:

┌───────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Before                                │ After                                                        │
├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ java/pom.xml — copilot-sdk-java (jar) │ java/pom.xml — copilot-sdk-java-parent (pom)                 │
├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                       │ java/sdk/pom.xml — copilot-sdk-java (jar)                    │
├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                       │ java/copilot-native/pom.xml — copilot-sdk-java-runtime (jar) │
└───────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Issues found in the workflows:

`java-publish-maven.yml`

1. `mvn help:evaluate -Dexpression=project.version` (Determine versions step) — now returns the parent POM version. This still works since parent and children share the same version, but it's worth noting.
2. `mvn release:prepare` / `release:perform` — runs from `./java` which is the parent. `release:prepare` will try to update versions in all 3 POMs and tag the release. The `tagNameFormat=java/v@{project.version}` should still work. However, `release:perform` with `-Dgoals="deploy"` will now deploy all reactor modules (parent pom, sdk jar, native-runtime jar). The parent POM has `<maven.deploy.skip>true</maven.deploy.skip>` — ✅ this correctly skips deploying the parent.
3. GitHub Release step hard-codes `ARTIFACT_ID="copilot-sdk-java"` — still correct for the main SDK artifact, but the release notes won't mention `copilot-sdk-java-runtime`. Consider adding it.
4. Doc update — `./scripts/update-documentation-versions.sh` may need to handle the new `copilot-native` module if its README references versions.

`java-publish-snapshot.yml`

5. `mvn help:evaluate -Dexpression=project.version` — same as above, returns parent version. Works but could be fragile if versions ever diverge.
6. `mvn -B deploy -DskipTests` — will now deploy all reactor modules. The parent POM's `<maven.deploy.skip>true</maven.deploy.skip>` prevents the parent from being deployed. The `copilot-sdk-java-runtime` module will be deployed as a snapshot — this is likely desired but should be confirmed.

Summary of required changes

The workflows are mostly compatible as-is because:

- Maven runs from `./java` (the reactor root) ✅
- Parent POM skips deploy ✅
- Version evaluation still works ✅

The main consideration is whether you want `copilot-sdk-java-runtime` published to Maven Central alongside `copilot-sdk-java`. If yes, no workflow changes needed. If the runtime module shouldn't be published yet, it needs a `<maven.deploy.skip>true</maven.deploy.skip>` in `copilot-native/pom.xml`.

2. The workflows themselves are structurally fine — `mvn release:prepare` and `mvn deploy` from the reactor root will process all modules. No workflow YAML changes are strictly needed, assuming the POM-level signing is fixed.

My proposal: Mark these as `@CopilotExperimental`:

1. `RuntimeConnection` (the whole sealed class) — the entire connection-selection API is new

4. The `ffi` package (`package-info.java` or each class) — all internal FFI plumbing is new and in-process-specific

The test uses `Path.of("").toAbsolutePath()` which resolves to the Maven module directory (`java/sdk/`), then looks for `README.md` there. Since we moved it to `java/`, we need to go up one level.

Fix: Change `root.resolve("README.md")` to `root.resolve("../README.md")` (or `root.getParent().resolve("README.md")`).

Now I have all the context I need to produce the table. Let me write the analysis.
Summary:
1. CopilotClient options (14387): The parameter `options` in `resolveInProcessEntrypoint` is genuinely unused — the method delegates to `NativeRuntimeLoader.resolveEntrypoint()` without using `options`. Valid. Could remove the parameter or use it.
2-12. JnaNativeBinding "useless parameters" (14302-14312): These are all parameters in the JNA `CopilotRuntimeLibrary` interface methods. JNA interface methods define the native function signature — they MUST have these parameters to match the C ABI. 
The Java-side implementation doesn't "use" them in Java code because JNA marshals them directly to the native call. Invalid — CodeQL doesn't understand JNA interfaces.
13-14. QueueInputStream offer (14275, 14276): `LinkedBlockingQueue.offer()` on an unbounded queue always succeeds, so ignoring its return value is safe. Invalid/debatable — the return is technically ignored, but it's fine in practice for unbounded queues.
15. NumberFormatException (14278): In test code, `Integer.parseInt` could throw if the Content-Length header value is malformed.
16. InProcessEnvGuard new String(String) (14375): Creating a new String instance with `"\0ABSENT\0"` is intentional — it's a sentinel pattern for identity comparison using `==`. Invalid.
